- **collapse-noise**: Use whenever a PR carries mechanical review noise alongside the real change — a mass formatter/linter pass (often hundreds of files), import-path-only edits from moving files between directories, or a concept rename carried through many files (shopCartId -&gt; basketId). Reviewers always want a clean diff, so apply this proactively after opening a PR or before requesting review on any such PR. Marks the noise-only files as "Viewed" via the GitHub GraphQL API so they collapse by default, leaving only the substantive files expanded in the Files Changed tab. - **collision-cross-section-prediction-integration**: Use when constructing a 4D (m/z, RT, CCS, fragment ion) reference database for N-Me derivatized unsaturated sterol lipids, or when CCS values for derivatized lipids must be predicted and merged with RT and MS/MS predictions for LC-IM-MS/MS identification. - **collision-cross-section-prediction-modeling**: Use when a spectral library (e.g. MS-DIAL .msp) contains RT or CCS annotations and you need structure-based predicted RT/CCS values plus quantified prediction quality (R²) against m/z- or logP-based baselines. - **collision-cross-section-regression**: Use when experimental CCS values for small molecules must be predicted or modeled from structures (e.g. AllCCS/BushCCS data), especially to test whether an MS/MS-pretrained encoder improves CCS regression over training from scratch. - **collision-cross-section-trajectory-calculation**: Use when gas-phase ion geometries and their DFT atomic charges are available and a CCS value is needed for comparison to TWIMS measurements or metabolite annotation. - **collision-energy-condition-comparison**: Use when evaluating whether in silico predicted spectra can rank the true compound from a candidate list filtered by precursor mass, and when comparing ranking accuracy across single versus merged collision-energy conditions. - **collision-energy-matched-fragment-annotation**: Use when both CFM-ID-predicted fragment lists and experimental HRMS/MS spectra (multi collision energy, e.g. 20/40/60 eV) exist for DNA adduct standards, and annotation confidence from fragment overlap is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/collision-energy-matched-fragment-annotation))
- **collision-energy-matching-and-stepped-spectrum-merging**: Use when evaluating simulated MS/MS spectra against challenge or reference sets whose acquisition used stepped collision energies or multiple CE replicate measurements, so predicted and experimental spectra must be compared on a consistent collision-energy basis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/collision-energy-matching-and-stepped-spectrum-merging))
